**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Ron's testimony states that he was at Lordly Tailor when the crime happened, and he claims to be Mask☆DeMasque. However, Evidence 11 indicates that Ron's wallet was found by Larry at his part-time job's company building (KB Security) on the night of the crime. Since Lordly Tailor is far from KB Security, these statements create a spatial contradiction.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Spatial Proposition Prop 1 applies to this situation because it deals with the impossibility of being in two distant locations simultaneously.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If Ron is inside Lordly Tailor when the crime happens and Lordly Tailor is far from the KB Security building, then Ron cannot be inside his part-time job's company building at the same time."

This proposition directly contradicts Ron's claim of being at Lordly Tailor when the crime occurred, given that his wallet was found at KB Security, indicating he was there at the time.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Since Lordly Tailor and KB Security are far apart, it is impossible for Ron to be physically present at both locations simultaneously. Therefore, if Ron's wallet was found at KB Security, he could not have been at Lordly Tailor at the same time.

## OBJECTIVE:
**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Adrian Andrews states in Testimony 5 that she knocked over the flower vase, causing it to fall onto the guitar case. However, Evidence 9 (Crime Photo) describes the scene where the remains of a smashed vase are found next to the guitar case, but only the top of the guitar case lid has water on it. This suggests that the vase did not directly fall onto the guitar case, but rather beside it.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand the contradiction better. Specifically, Prop 5 deals with the spatial relationship between the flower vase and Corrida, but it does not directly address the spatial relationship between the flower vase and the guitar case.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

None of the listed propositions directly apply to the contradiction identified.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial description of the objects in the crime scene. According to Andrews' testimony, the vase fell onto the guitar case, implying that the water from the vase would likely soak the entire guitar case. However, the Crime Photo indicates that the water only affected the top of the guitar case lid, suggesting that the vase fell beside the guitar case rather than directly onto it.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 9, "testimony": 5}

PARSED RESULT:
--------------------
{
  "evidence": 9,
  "testimony": 5
}

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 4 does apply. According to Phoenix's testimony, Doug Swallow fell on his umbrella, implying that the umbrella was intact at the time of the fall. However, the umbrella being broken near an electrical pole contradicts this statement since it suggests that the umbrella broke after the fall, possibly due to another impact or event.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Spatial Propositions 1 and 5 are relevant to the scenario described by Ini Miney.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

- **Proposition 1:** If Ini Miney is in the Side Room and walks to the Channeling Chamber, Ini Miney must pass through the hallway.
- **Proposition 5:** If Ini Miney did not see anyone on the way to the Channeling Chamber and Ini Miney is in the hallway, then no one is in the hallway.

Ini Miney's testimony (Testimony 4) states that she did not see anyone on the way to the Channeling Chamber. According to Proposition 1, she would have passed through the hallway. Therefore, Proposition 5 implies that no one was in the hallway when Ini Miney walked through it.

However, Evidence 14 states that the Sacred Urn was under repair in the hallway, which means someone (the person repairing the Sacred Urn) was in the hallway. This contradicts Ini Miney's statement that she did not see anyone on the way to the Channeling Chamber.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises because Ini Miney's claim of not seeing anyone while traveling from the Side Room to the Channeling Chamber conflicts with the fact that the Sacred Urn was under repair in the hallway, implying that a person was present in the hallway during her journey.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

This means the fire started at 10:45 PM and ended around 11:15 PM.

However, the shack's distance from Dusky Bridge is not specified, but it is implied that Butz could see the fire from the shack.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Since the shack is a 5-minute walk from Eagle Bridge, it is reasonable to assume that it is also relatively close to Dusky Bridge. If Butz was inside the shack at 10:30 PM and the fire started at 10:45 PM, he would have had to leave the shack and travel to Dusky Bridge to be part of the tracks leading from the Main Gate to Dusky Bridge. **Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, the spatial propositions do apply and help us identify the contradiction. The spatial arrangement described in Evidence 6 contradicts Fawles's statement in Testimony 0 about stopping his car in front of the bridge and waiting on the bridge.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the provided evidence and testimonies, we need to look for inconsistencies in the spatial descriptions. Evidence 2 (Crime Photo 1) and Evidence 7 (Crime Photo 2) both describe the position of the hat relative to the victim's forehead. According to Evidence 2, the hat covers the victim's eyes and forehead, while Evidence 7 indicates that the hat has been removed during the investigation, exposing the victim's forehead.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 3 states: "If the Crime Photo 2 shows the victim's forehead and the hat is removed, then the hat must have been covering the victim's forehead in Crime Photo 1." However, since the hat is removed in Crime Photo 2, this does not inherently create a contradiction unless there is additional information suggesting the hat was not originally covering the forehead in Crime Photo 1.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Upon further review, there is no direct contradiction based on the spatial propositions alone. However, the contradiction arises from the testimonies and the evidence descriptions. Testimony 5 mentions that the defendant grabs the bottle from the table, implying an action that would likely displace the bottle. Evidence 3 (Deadly Bottle) confirms that the bottle was indeed used as the murder weapon and bears the defendant's prints, but it does not specify its location post-grab. Therefore, the contradiction lies in the spatial proposition Prop 1, which states that if the Deadly Bottle is on the table and Olga's Photo shows the defendant grabbing it, then the Deadly Bottle is no longer on the table after the grab. Since Crime Photo 1 still shows the bottle on the table, this contradicts the implication of Testimony 5.

This suggests that the shooter was positioned below the victim, as the bullet would have had to travel upward to enter the lower abdomen and exit the shoulder.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can use Proposition 2 to analyze the spatial relationship described in the testimonies and evidence. Proposition 2 states that if the Victim is shot from above and the bullet exits through the right shoulder, then the Victim falls backward.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial positioning implied by the bullet trajectory in Evidence 6 and the spatial positioning described in Testimony 2. The bullet entered the lower right abdomen and exited the right shoulder, indicating the shooter was below the victim. **Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Atishon states that Dr. Buff was standing in front of the bookshelves and was struck on the head from behind by Datz Are'bal (Testimony 2 and Testimony 3). This implies that Datz Are'bal was behind the bookshelves when he attacked Dr. Buff.

However, Evidence 3, the Study Photo, shows that Dr. Buff is lying next to the floor-to-ceiling windows and is covered by books. According to Evidence 4, the Study Diagram, the bookshelf is on the other side of the window. This suggests that Dr. Buff was closer to the window than the bookshelf, which contradicts Atishon's testimony that Dr. Buff was standing in front of the bookshelves.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Propositions 1, 4, and 5 involve positions relative to the window and bookshelves, which are relevant to the contradiction identified.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 4 states: "If Dr. Buff is struck on the head from behind by Datz Are'bal and Dr. Buff is facing the bookshelves, then Datz Are'bal is behind the bookshelves."

This proposition supports Atishon's testimony that Datz Are'bal was behind the bookshelves when he attacked Dr. Buff. However, the Study Photo and Study Diagram suggest that Dr. Buff was near the window rather than in front of the bookshelves, which would mean Datz Are'bal could not have been behind the bookshelves when he attacked Dr. Buff.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Atishon's testimony places Dr. Buff in front of the bookshelves, which would put Datz Are'bal behind the bookshelves. However, the Study Photo and Study Diagram indicate that Dr. Buff was near the window, which is opposite to the bookshelves. Therefore, Datz Are'bal could not have been behind the bookshelves when he attacked Dr. Buff, contradicting Atishon's testimony.

**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Shih-na's testimonies indicate that she was in the next room over when she heard the scream (Testimony 2). According to Evidence 29, the Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall and into the next room over. This suggests that the Yatagarasu was in the same room as the fireplace wall when escaping.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If Shih-na was in the next room over when she heard the scream, then she is behind the Yatagarasu if Yatagarasu was in front of the fireplace wall."

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 applies here. If Shih-na was in the next room over when she heard the scream, and the Yatagarasu was in front of the fireplace wall when escaping, then Shih-na should have been behind the Yatagarasu. However, Shih-na's testimony (Testimony 3) states that she was unable to catch a glimpse of the Yatagarasu despite being in the next room over. This implies that the Yatagarasu was not in a position where Shih-na could see them, which contradicts the spatial arrangement suggested by Proposition 1.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Spatial reasoning indicates that if Shih-na was in the next room over and the Yatagarasu was in front of the fireplace wall, Shih-na should have been able to see the Yatagarasu as it escaped through the revolving fireplace wall. Since Shih-na claims she did not see the Yatagarasu, this contradicts the spatial proposition that places her behind the Yatagarasu.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 29, "testimony": 3}

PARSED RESULT:
--------------------
{
  "evidence": 29,
  "testimony": 3
}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Nash and Ringo Skulkin claim that they were inside the pawnbrokery when they heard the gunshot, which made them jump and caused items to go flying. They also mention that they put everything back and left the storeroom, running into the man in black at the exit.

The evidence suggests that Gina Lestrade was found unconscious with Windibank's gun, and there is a single bullet wound in the victim's back, indicating that the gunshot came from behind him. Additionally, the floor plan (Evidence 11) and the photographs (Evidence 14 and Evidence 15) provide information about the layout and the scene of the crime.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can use Propositions 1, 2, and 5 to analyze the spatial aspects of the testimonies and evidence.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 2 states that if the Skulkin brothers are inside the pawnbrokery when they hear the gunshot, then they are inside the storeroom or main shop. Given that the Skulkins heard the gunshot and were startled, this proposition supports their claim of being inside the pawnbrokery.

Proposition 5 states that if the gunshot makes the Skulkin brothers jump and all that stuff goes flying, then the items that were knocked over are originally inside the storeroom. This aligns with Ringo's testimony that the gunshot made them jump and items went flying.

However, Proposition 1 states that if Windibank is holding the gun and the gunshot came from behind him, then the shooter is behind Windibank. This proposition conflicts with the Skulkin brothers' testimonies because if they were inside the storeroom or main shop when the gunshot occurred, they would have been startled by the sound, but they could not have been the shooters since they claim they were not rifling for anything and were startled themselves.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 does apply and reveals the contradiction. According to the evidence, Windibank was holding the gun and the gunshot came from behind him, implying the shooter was outside the area where Windibank was standing. However, the Skulkin brothers claim they were inside the pawnbrokery and were startled by the gunshot, suggesting they were not the shooters.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 10, "testimony": 6}

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, we understand that Gumshoe was standing guard in front of Lobby No. 2 and did not move from the Lobby No. 2 door until he heard the gunshot. This implies that Gumshoe was positioned outside Lobby No. 2 when the gunshot occurred.

However, Evidence 6 (Plastic Bag) states that it has Faraday's blood on it and was found in Lobby No. 2. According to Testimony 3, Gumshoe did not move from the Lobby No. 2 door until he heard the gunshot. This suggests that Gumshoe would have been guarding the entrance to Lobby No. 2 when the gunshot happened, meaning the Plastic Bag with Faraday's blood would be behind him relative to the interior of Lobby No. 2.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify a contradiction based on the spatial relationships described.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Spatial Proposition 5 applies here: "If the Plastic Bag with Faraday's blood is in Lobby No. 2 and Gumshoe was guarding the entrance to Lobby No. 2, then the Plastic Bag is behind Gumshoe relative to the interior of Lobby No. 2."

This proposition supports the idea that if Gumshoe was indeed guarding the entrance to Lobby No. 2 without moving, the Plastic Bag with Faraday's blood would be located behind him relative to the interior of Lobby No. 2. However, this contradicts the fact that Gumshoe did not enter Lobby No. 2 and thus could not have interacted with the Plastic Bag inside.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 5 does apply and helps us identify the contradiction. The contradiction arises because if Gumshoe was guarding the entrance to Lobby No. 2 and did not move, he would be outside the lobby, making it impossible for him to have placed or interacted with the Plastic Bag inside Lobby No. 2.
